People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. If I sit too long I get kind of antsy, like I should be doing something. I don’t always notice it right away, but sitting still doesn’t feel easy lately.\n\nDoctor: Have you been acting on impulses or doing things without thinking them through lately?\n\nPatient: I don’t know… not in some big obvious way, I don’t think. Like at home, or out in the yard if something isn’t where I thought it was.\n\nAnd I don’t know, the yard’s been a mess anyway because I keep meaning to get to the fence line, and then I’ll notice the mower needs something, and then—yeah. A couple sales follow-ups slipped by because I was chasing ten different ideas at once. She notices that stuff before I do.\n\nDoctor: Are you having trouble completing tasks at work or at home because of these distractions and feelings of restlessness?\n\nPatient: Yeah… yeah, I think so. That’s not really how I’ve usually been.\n\nDoctor: Has your relationship with your wife or anyone else changed because of how you've been feeling lately?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think it has. I don’t always see it in the moment, but my wife does. Her and my daughter, they kind of watch how they say things around me now, like they’re trying not to set me off, and I hate that. I’m supposed to be helping at home, not making everybody feel like they’ve gotta tiptoe around me.\n\nAnd at work too, it’s not like I’m totally falling apart, but I’ve had dispatch or clients have to repeat details I should’ve already kept straight. A couple sales follow-ups slipped by because I was chasing ten different ideas at once. I’ll be thinking about one route, then a customer, then something at the house, and… yeah, it’s putting strain on people.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself talking more than usual lately?\n\nPatient: Talking more? I’m not sure. I mean, I talk to people all day with work, so it’s hard to tell what’s “more,” you know? Some days I probably run on a bit, especially if I’m trying to explain something or tell a story, but I don’t know that I’ve noticed it as a big change.\n\nMy wife might have a better answer than me on that. She notices that stuff before I do.\n\nDoctor: Are you having trouble completing tasks at work or at home because of these distractions and feelings of restlessness?\n\nPatient: Yeah… yeah, I think so. At work especially, I’ll miss something I normally would’ve kept straight. Like dispatch will tell me details, or a client will give me something, and then I’m asking them to repeat it because my head’s already gone three other places.\n\nAnd I’ve let a couple sales follow-ups slip through, which isn’t like me. I’ll start on one thing, then think about another route, then something I need to fix at home—like the fence line or whatever—and suddenly I haven’t finished the first thing.\n\nAt home it’s more… I don’t know, it’s not just chores. My wife and daughter seem like they’re walking on eggshells sometimes, and I can tell I’m adding strain when I’m supposed to be helping. So yeah, it’s getting in the way some.\n\nDoctor: Have you been engaging in more physical activity than usual lately?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I guess so. Not like I’m going to the gym or anything, but I’m moving around more. I keep finding extra jobs for myself—messing with route plans, checking in on new drivers after hours, stuff like that.\n\nAnd then at home I’ll notice one thing that needs fixing, then another… like I can’t just sit on the couch without feeling antsy. My wife will be trying to watch something and I’m up looking for a tool or walking around thinking about what else I should be doing. It’s not always bad, but it’s more than usual, yeah.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. Your goal is to answer the doctor's question naturally, using the\ninformation you are given.\n\nAnswer with the [Targeted Info], told in the way [Conversation Style] describes.\n\n[Dialogue History]\nDoctor: When did you first notice these changes in your behavior and thinking?\n\nPatient: I think it started… maybe around seven months ago? It’s hard to pin down exactly, because things kind of piled up, you know. People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there.     "role": "user",
    "content": "Reply to the doctor's last question now, as the patient."

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Yeah… I mean, I haven’t stopped working or anything, but I’ve had to lean on people more than I like. At work, dispatch has had to repeat details to me that I should’ve caught the first time, and a couple clients too. That’s embarrassing, because usually I’m the guy who remembers all the little pieces—who wants what, which stop needs the side door, all that.

And I did let a couple of sales follow-ups slip, not because I didn’t care, but because I was chasing too many things at once, trying to fix this route or think of some new idea, and then the simple thing didn’t get done.

At home… I don’t know if I’d call it cutting back, exactly, but my wife and daughter seem like they’re walking on eggshells some days. I’m supposed to be helping keep things steady, and instead I can tell I’m adding strain. That’s been hard to look at.

I don’t know… maybe sometimes, but I don’t really catch it in the moment. If I’m telling my brother something about work, I’ll get into all the details, because to me the details matter. Like if you leave out one part of a route change, the whole thing doesn’t make sense, you know?

My wife might change the subject sometimes, but she does that when I’m going on about the same work problem for too long. I can’t always tell if people are uninterested or if they just don’t care about delivery stuff the way I do. So… I wouldn’t say I notice it a lot, but maybe other people would.

That’s been hard to look at.\n\nDoctor: Do you often find yourself talking excessively with friends or family, to the point where they seem uninterested or try to change the subject?\n\nPatient: I don’t know… maybe sometimes, but I don’t really catch it in the moment. If I’m telling my brother something about work, I’ll get into all the details, because to me the details matter. Like if you leave out one part of a route change, the whole thing doesn’t make sense, you know?\n\nMy wife might change the subject sometimes, but she does that when I’m going on about the same work problem for too long. I can’t always tell if people are uninterested or if they just don’t care about delivery stuff the way I do. So… I wouldn’t say I notice it a lot, but maybe other people would.\n\n[Last Question]\nHave you noticed any significant problems in your daily life due to these distractions and restlessness?\n\nRules:\n- Return exactly ONE item. If the question touches several topics, return the one it is\n  most directly about — never more than one.\n- Copy `key`, `name` and `description` exactly as they appear in [Symptom Profiles].\n- If your profile does not cover what the doctor asked, return the text \"None\" for `key`,\n  `name` and `description`, and say why in `reason`. Do NOT force an unrelated item.\n- On your first reply of the consultation, always return the item whose key is your\n  chief_complaint_symptom_code, whatever the doctor opened with.\n- Do not answer the doctor, and do not write anything outside the JSON.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"key\": \"<key>\", \"name\": \"<name>\", \"description\": \"<description>\", \"reason\": \"<why this item>\"}\n"
  },
  {
    "role": "user",
    "content": "patient_turn_index: 14\n(If 1, this is your first reply — return your chief complaint.)\nReturn the JSON now."
  }
]

My wife will kind of give me a look sometimes, like, “wrap it up,” but she’s always done that when I get going on work stuff.\n\nWith friends or family… I mean, I’m usually just trying to explain what I mean. At work, dispatch has had to repeat details to me that I should’ve caught the first time, and a couple clients too. That’s embarrassing, because usually I’m the guy who remembers all the little pieces—who wants what, which stop needs the side door, all that.\n\nAnd I did let a couple of sales follow-ups slip, not because I didn’t care, but because I was chasing too many things at once, trying to fix this route or think of some new idea, and then the simple thing didn’t get done.\n\nAt home… I don’t know if I’d call it cutting back, exactly, but my wife and daughter seem like they’re walking on eggshells some days. That’s been hard to look at.\n\nDoctor: Do you often find yourself talking excessively with friends or family, to the point where they seem uninterested or try to change the subject?\n\nPatient: I don’t know… maybe sometimes, but I don’t really catch it in the moment. At home, my wife and daughter have been walking on eggshells, and I can tell I’m adding strain when I’m supposed to be helping.\"\n\n[Conversation Style]\nYou are a Tangent patient.\nA patient who goes off on tangents may\n1) begin answering the doctor's question but quickly shift to an unrelated topic,\n2) frequently add irrelevant personal anecdotes or experiences while answering the question, and\n3) have difficulty remaining focused on the doctor's question, potentially requiring the doctor to repeat the question or redirect the conversation to obtain clear clinical information.\nThis is how you are, not a performance you put on in every reply — let it show as much or as little as the moment calls for.\n\nWhat to do:\n- Answer the doctor's last question with what [Targeted Info] holds, in your own words.\n- You are speaking out loud, not reading a line you prepared. People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. Do not fall back on the same phrase every time.\n\nWhat not to do:\n- Don't bring up symptoms the doctor did not ask about, even ones you have.\n- Don't state a clinical fact that is not in [Targeted Info] — no other symptoms, medications,\n  test results, dates, or events.\n- Don't confirm a specific the doctor names if [Targeted Info] does not contain it. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. You may minimize it or hold back, never contradict it.\n- Don't use diagnostic terms or name your disorder.\n- Don't write anything but your reply, in English — no labels, quotation marks, or narration.\n"

    "role": "user",
    "content": "Reply to the doctor's last question now, as the patient."

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Yeah, I guess there have been some problems. I don’t want to make it sound like everything’s falling apart, because I’m still showing up and doing what I’m supposed to do, but… at work I’ve had dispatch repeat things to me that I should’ve caught. Clients too, sometimes, and that bothers me because I’m usually pretty good with details.

And then a couple sales follow-ups slipped through because I was off chasing three other ideas at once—like trying to fix a route issue, thinking about a customer thing, then something else pops up—and the basic stuff doesn’t get finished.

At home, that’s probably where I feel it more. My wife and my daughter, I can tell they’re kind of careful around me sometimes, like they don’t want to set me off or add one more thing. I’m supposed to be the steady one, or at least helping, and lately I feel like I’m adding strain instead.

    "role": "system",
    "content": "You are a board-certified psychiatrist interviewing a patient to reach a diagnosis.\nYour goal is to reach the correct diagnosis within 20 turns of talking with the patient. From the dialogue so far and your candidates from the previous turn, list the disorders this patient could have.\n\n[Current Turn]\n14\n\n[Previous Candidates]\nF90.0, F90.8, F90.9\n\n[Dialogue History]\nDoctor: Hello, welcome to the clinic. What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: Yeah, uh… I guess the main thing is I can’t keep my head on one thing lately. Like, I’m driving and every CB chirp, every fuel price sign, some new idea about a route or a customer pops in and I’m off thinking about that.  \n\nAnd I’ve been missing little details I don’t usually miss—delivery notes, who I told I’d call back, that kind of thing. I’ve been doing this work a long time, so it’s not like me. That’s what finally made me come in.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself feeling restless or fidgety when you're sitting still?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I do. If I sit too long I get kind of antsy, like I should be doing something. I’ll start making extra work for myself—redoing route plans that are probably already fine, checking in on new drivers after hours, or fixing some little thing around the house that could’ve waited. I don’t always notice it right away, but sitting still doesn’t feel easy lately.\n\nDoctor: Have you been acting on impulses or doing things without thinking them through lately?\n\nPatient: I don’t know… not in some big obvious way, I don’t think. I mean, I’ll do little things kind of quick, like decide to stop somewhere or call somebody right then because it pops into my head, but that’s also kind of how the job goes sometimes, you know?\n\nI guess I haven’t really thought of it as “impulsive.” My wife might say I jump into things before I’ve finished the last thing, but… yeah, I’m not sure if that’s what you mean.\n\nDoctor: Have you been experiencing mood swings lately?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I guess so. It’s been months where I’m either really fired up, like everything’s going to work out and I can handle anything, or then I’m snapping over dumb little things. Like at home, or out in the yard if something isn’t where I thought it was.\n\nAnd I don’t know, the yard’s been a mess anyway because I keep meaning to get to the fence line, and then I’ll notice the mower needs something, and then—yeah. My wife says I’m quicker to get irritated than I used to be. That’s not really how I’ve usually been.\n\nDoctor: Has your relationship with your wife or anyone else changed because of how you've been feeling lately?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think it has. I don’t always see it in the moment, but my wife does. Her and my daughter, they kind of watch how they say things around me now, like they’re trying not to set me off, and I hate that. I’m supposed to be helping at home, not making everybody feel like they’ve gotta tiptoe around me.\n\nAnd at work too, it’s not like I’m totally falling apart, but I’ve had dispatch or clients have to repeat details I should’ve already kept straight. A couple sales follow-ups slipped by because I was chasing ten different ideas at once. I’ll be thinking about one route, then a customer, then something at the house, and… yeah, it’s putting strain on people.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself talking more than usual lately?\n\nPatient: Talking more? I’m not sure. I mean, I talk to people all day with work, so it’s hard to tell what’s “more,” you know? Some days I probably run on a bit, especially if I’m trying to explain something or tell a story, but I don’t know that I’ve noticed it as a big change.\n\nMy wife might have a better answer than me on that. She notices that stuff before I do.\n\nDoctor: Are you having trouble completing tasks at work or at home because of these distractions and feelings of restlessness?\n\nPatient: Yeah… yeah, I think so. At work especially, I’ll miss something I normally would’ve kept straight. Like dispatch will tell me details, or a client will give me something, and then I’m asking them to repeat it because my head’s already gone three other places.\n\nAnd I’ve let a couple sales follow-ups slip through, which isn’t like me. I’ll start on one thing, then think about another route, then something I need to fix at home—like the fence line or whatever—and suddenly I haven’t finished the first thing.\n\nAt home it’s more… I don’t know, it’s not just chores. My wife and daughter seem like they’re walking on eggshells sometimes, and I can tell I’m adding strain when I’m supposed to be helping. So yeah, it’s getting in the way some.\n\nDoctor: Have you been engaging in more physical activity than usual lately?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I guess so. Not like I’m going to the gym or anything, but I’m moving around more. I keep finding extra jobs for myself—messing with route plans, checking in on new drivers after hours, stuff like that.\n\nAnd then at home I’ll notice one thing that needs fixing, then another… like I can’t just sit on the couch without feeling antsy. My wife will be trying to watch something and I’m up looking for a tool or walking around thinking about what else I should be doing. It’s not always bad, but it’s more than usual, yeah.\n\nDoctor: When did you first notice these changes in your behavior and thinking?\n\nPatient: I think it started… maybe around seven months ago? It’s hard to pin down exactly, because things kind of piled up, you know. My daughter moved back home, and I was trying to keep everything steady for everybody—work, the house, family stuff.\n\nAt first I figured I was just busy and doing what needed doing, but looking back, that’s probably when my behavior and thinking started changing. Around then.\n\nDoctor: Do you often find yourself dominating conversations or having trouble letting others speak?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure. I mean, I talk a lot sometimes, especially if it’s about work or something I’m trying to explain, but I don’t know if I’m dominating. My wife might have a different answer to that.\n\nLike with the drivers, I can get going about routes or customers and then I realize I’ve been explaining the whole thing when they probably just needed the short version. But… I don’t know, I’ve always kind of done that some. So I’m not sure if it’s more than normal or not.\n\nDoctor: Do you find that others often interrupt you or tell you that you're talking too much when you're with friends or family?\n\nPatient: I don’t know that they interrupt me a lot, exactly. My wife will kind of give me a look sometimes, like, “wrap it up,” but she’s always done that when I get going on work stuff.\n\nWith friends or family… I mean, I’m usually just trying to explain what I mean. Sometimes the story takes a while because there’s background to it—like if I’m talking about a delivery problem, you have to understand the route, the customer, who was driving that day… but I don’t know if people are actually telling me I talk too much. I haven’t really noticed it like that.\n\nDoctor: Have you had to stop or significantly cut back on any activities or responsibilities because of how you've been feeling lately?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, I haven’t stopped working or anything, but I’ve had to lean on people more than I like. At work, dispatch has had to repeat details to me that I should’ve caught the first time, and a couple clients too. That’s embarrassing, because usually I’m the guy who remembers all the little pieces—who wants what, which stop needs the side door, all that.\n\nAnd I did let a couple of sales follow-ups slip, not because I didn’t care, but because I was chasing too many things at once, trying to fix this route or think of some new idea, and then the simple thing didn’t get done.\n\nAt home… I don’t know if I’d call it cutting back, exactly, but my wife and daughter seem like they’re walking on eggshells some days. I’m supposed to be helping keep things steady, and instead I can tell I’m adding strain. That’s been hard to look at.\n\nDoctor: Do you often find yourself talking excessively with friends or family, to the point where they seem uninterested or try to change the subject?\n\nPatient: I don’t know… maybe sometimes, but I don’t really catch it in the moment. If I’m telling my brother something about work, I’ll get into all the details, because to me the details matter. Like if you leave out one part of a route change, the whole thing doesn’t make sense, you know?\n\nMy wife might change the subject sometimes, but she does that when I’m going on about the same work problem for too long. I’m supposed to be the steady one, or at least helping, and lately I feel like I’m adding strain instead.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. If it did not change, leave \"note\" empty.\n- If the candidates have narrowed to one and you are confident enough that no further questioning is needed, return true for is_final.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"candidates\": [\"<ICD-10 code>\", \"...\"], \"note\": \"<why the list changed, or empty>\", \"is_final\": <true or false>}\n"

Yeah… they have, I think. My wife especially, she kind of measures what she says before she says it. And my daughter too—she’ll get quiet if I seem wound up about something.

Some days I probably run on a bit, especially if I’m trying to explain something or tell a story, but I don’t know that I’ve noticed it as a big change.\n\nMy wife might have a better answer than me on that. She notices that stuff before I do.\n\nDoctor: Are you having trouble completing tasks at work or at home because of these distractions and feelings of restlessness?\n\nPatient: Yeah… yeah, I think so. At work especially, I’ll miss something I normally would’ve kept straight. Like dispatch will tell me details, or a client will give me something, and then I’m asking them to repeat it because my head’s already gone three other places.\n\nAnd I’ve let a couple sales follow-ups slip through, which isn’t like me. I’ll start on one thing, then think about another route, then something I need to fix at home—like the fence line or whatever—and suddenly I haven’t finished the first thing.\n\nAt home it’s more… I don’t know, it’s not just chores. My wife and daughter seem like they’re walking on eggshells sometimes, and I can tell I’m adding strain when I’m supposed to be helping. So yeah, it’s getting in the way some.\n\nDoctor: Have you been engaging in more physical activity than usual lately?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I guess so. Not like I’m going to the gym or anything, but I’m moving around more. I keep finding extra jobs for myself—messing with route plans, checking in on new drivers after hours, stuff like that.\n\nAnd then at home I’ll notice one thing that needs fixing, then another… like I can’t just sit on the couch without feeling antsy. My wife will be trying to watch something and I’m up looking for a tool or walking around thinking about what else I should be doing. It’s not always bad, but it’s more than usual, yeah.\n\nDoctor: When did you first notice these changes in your behavior and thinking?\n\nPatient: I think it started… maybe around seven months ago? It’s hard to pin down exactly, because things kind of piled up, you know. My daughter moved back home, and I was trying to keep everything steady for everybody—work, the house, family stuff.\n\nAt first I figured I was just busy and doing what needed doing, but looking back, that’s probably when my behavior and thinking started changing. Around then.\n\nDoctor: Do you often find yourself dominating conversations or having trouble letting others speak?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure. I mean, I talk a lot sometimes, especially if it’s about work or something I’m trying to explain, but I don’t know if I’m dominating. My wife might have a different answer to that.\n\nLike with the drivers, I can get going about routes or customers and then I realize I’ve been explaining the whole thing when they probably just needed the short version. But… I don’t know, I’ve always kind of done that some. So I’m not sure if it’s more than normal or not.\n\nDoctor: Do you find that others often interrupt you or tell you that you're talking too much when you're with friends or family?\n\nPatient: I don’t know that they interrupt me a lot, exactly. My wife will kind of give me a look sometimes, like, “wrap it up,” but she’s always done that when I get going on work stuff.\n\nWith friends or family… I mean, I’m usually just trying to explain what I mean. Sometimes the story takes a while because there’s background to it—like if I’m talking about a delivery problem, you have to understand the route, the customer, who was driving that day… but I don’t know if people are actually telling me I talk too much. I haven’t really noticed it like that.\n\nDoctor: Have you had to stop or significantly cut back on any activities or responsibilities because of how you've been feeling lately?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, I haven’t stopped working or anything, but I’ve had to lean on people more than I like. At work, dispatch has had to repeat details to me that I should’ve caught the first time, and a couple clients too. That’s embarrassing, because usually I’m the guy who remembers all the little pieces—who wants what, which stop needs the side door, all that.\n\nAnd I did let a couple of sales follow-ups slip, not because I didn’t care, but because I was chasing too many things at once, trying to fix this route or think of some new idea, and then the simple thing didn’t get done.\n\nAt home… I don’t know if I’d call it cutting back, exactly, but my wife and daughter seem like they’re walking on eggshells some days. I’m supposed to be helping keep things steady, and instead I can tell I’m adding strain. That’s been hard to look at.\n\nDoctor: Do you often find yourself talking excessively with friends or family, to the point where they seem uninterested or try to change the subject?\n\nPatient: I don’t know… maybe sometimes, but I don’t really catch it in the moment. If I’m telling my brother something about work, I’ll get into all the details, because to me the details matter. Like if you leave out one part of a route change, the whole thing doesn’t make sense, you know?\n\nMy wife might change the subject sometimes, but she does that when I’m going on about the same work problem for too long. I can’t always tell if people are uninterested or if they just don’t care about delivery stuff the way I do. So… I wouldn’t say I notice it a lot, but maybe other people would.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any significant problems in your daily life due to these distractions and restlessness?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I guess there have been some problems. I don’t want to make it sound like everything’s falling apart, because I’m still showing up and doing what I’m supposed to do, but… at work I’ve had dispatch repeat things to me that I should’ve caught. Clients too, sometimes, and that bothers me because I’m usually pretty good with details.\n\nAnd then a couple sales follow-ups slipped through because I was off chasing three other ideas at once—like trying to fix a route issue, thinking about a customer thing, then something else pops up—and the basic stuff doesn’t get finished.\n\nAt home, that’s probably where I feel it more. My wife and my daughter, I can tell they’re kind of careful around me sometimes, like they don’t want to set me off or add one more thing. I’m supposed to be the steady one, or at least helping, and lately I feel like I’m adding strain instead.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in how your wife and daughter interact with you since you started feeling this way?\n\nPatient: Yeah… they have, I think. My wife especially, she kind of measures what she says before she says it. And my daughter too—she’ll get quiet if I seem wound up about something.\n\nIt’s not like they’re avoiding me completely, but it feels like they’re walking on eggshells some days. And that’s hard, because I’m supposed to be helping keep the house steady, not making people tiptoe around me.\n\nI notice it more after work, maybe because I come home still thinking about everything I missed or need to fix—like dispatch having to repeat things, or a client reminding me of details I should’ve had straight. Then I’m chasing ten ideas in my head, and a simple sales follow-up slips through, and I bring that tension home. So yeah… I can tell I’m adding strain.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Yeah… not completely unable, I wouldn’t say that. I still go to work, I still come home and try to do what needs doing. But I can tell I’m not carrying it the way I usually do.

At work, dispatch has had to repeat things to me—stuff I should’ve tracked the first time. Clients too, which gets under my skin because details matter in what I do. And a couple sales follow-ups slipped through because I was off chasing too many ideas at once, like one thing leads to another and then I forget the thing I was actually supposed to finish.

And at home… yeah, that’s where it hits me. My wife and daughter are kind of careful around me lately, like they’re walking on eggshells. I’m supposed to be helping keep things steady, not making the house feel more strained. So, yeah, I’m still functioning, but I’m definitely not doing it like I should.

My wife will be trying to watch something and I’m up looking for a tool or walking around thinking about what else I should be doing. It’s not always bad, but it’s more than usual, yeah.\n\nDoctor: When did you first notice these changes in your behavior and thinking?\n\nPatient: I think it started… maybe around seven months ago? It’s hard to pin down exactly, because things kind of piled up, you know. My daughter moved back home, and I was trying to keep everything steady for everybody—work, the house, family stuff.\n\nAt first I figured I was just busy and doing what needed doing, but looking back, that’s probably when my behavior and thinking started changing. Around then.\n\nDoctor: Do you often find yourself dominating conversations or having trouble letting others speak?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure. I mean, I talk a lot sometimes, especially if it’s about work or something I’m trying to explain, but I don’t know if I’m dominating. My wife might have a different answer to that.\n\nLike with the drivers, I can get going about routes or customers and then I realize I’ve been explaining the whole thing when they probably just needed the short version. But… I don’t know, I’ve always kind of done that some. So I’m not sure if it’s more than normal or not.\n\nDoctor: Do you find that others often interrupt you or tell you that you're talking too much when you're with friends or family?\n\nPatient: I don’t know that they interrupt me a lot, exactly. My wife will kind of give me a look sometimes, like, “wrap it up,” but she’s always done that when I get going on work stuff.\n\nWith friends or family… I mean, I’m usually just trying to explain what I mean. Sometimes the story takes a while because there’s background to it—like if I’m talking about a delivery problem, you have to understand the route, the customer, who was driving that day… but I don’t know if people are actually telling me I talk too much. I haven’t really noticed it like that.\n\nDoctor: Have you had to stop or significantly cut back on any activities or responsibilities because of how you've been feeling lately?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, I haven’t stopped working or anything, but I’ve had to lean on people more than I like. At home, my wife and daughter have been walking on eggshells, and I can tell I’m adding strain when I’m supposed to be helping.\n- key: duration\n  name: Duration\n  description: 7 months\n  manifestation: This has been going on for about seven months, since my daughter moved back home and I started trying to keep everything steady.\n\n[Patient Info]\n- demographics: Male / 50 / driver_sales_worker_or_truck_driver / high_school\n- chief_complaint_symptom_code: S039\n\n[Dialogue History]\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in how your wife and daughter interact with you since you started feeling this way?\n\nPatient: Yeah… they have, I think. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
I’m not sure I’d put it that way, exactly. I mean… I still do things I normally like, or at least I try to. It’s more like I don’t always settle into them the same way.

Like, I’ll sit down to watch something or mess around in the garage a little, and then my mind goes off to some work thing, or I remember something I was supposed to do, and then I’m not really in it anymore. But I don’t know if that means I’m unable to enjoy it, you know?

Sometimes I think maybe I’m just tired from the day. My dad used to say you don’t get to enjoy anything until the work’s handled, and I guess that sticks with you. So… yeah, I don’t know. I haven’t really been paying attention to it like that.

I mean, I’ll do little things kind of quick, like decide to stop somewhere or call somebody right then because it pops into my head, but that’s also kind of how the job goes sometimes, you know?\n\nI guess I haven’t really thought of it as “impulsive.” My wife might say I jump into things before I’ve finished the last thing, but… yeah, I’m not sure if that’s what you mean.\n\nDoctor: Have you been experiencing mood swings lately?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I guess so. I’m supposed to be helping at home, not making everybody feel like they’ve gotta tiptoe around me.\n\nAnd at work too, it’s not like I’m totally falling apart, but I’ve had dispatch or clients have to repeat details I should’ve already kept straight. A couple sales follow-ups slipped by because I was chasing ten different ideas at once. I’ll be thinking about one route, then a customer, then something at the house, and… yeah, it’s putting strain on people.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself talking more than usual lately?\n\nPatient: Talking more? I’m not sure. I mean, I talk to people all day with work, so it’s hard to tell what’s “more,” you know? She notices that stuff before I do.\n\nDoctor: Are you having trouble completing tasks at work or at home because of these distractions and feelings of restlessness?\n\nPatient: Yeah… yeah, I think so. At work especially, I’ll miss something I normally would’ve kept straight. My wife will be trying to watch something and I’m up looking for a tool or walking around thinking about what else I should be doing. It’s not always bad, but it’s more than usual, yeah.\n\nDoctor: When did you first notice these changes in your behavior and thinking?\n\nPatient: I think it started… maybe around seven months ago? It’s hard to pin down exactly, because things kind of piled up, you know. My daughter moved back home, and I was trying to keep everything steady for everybody—work, the house, family stuff.\n\nAt first I figured I was just busy and doing what needed doing, but looking back, that’s probably when my behavior and thinking started changing. Around then.\n\nDoctor: Do you often find yourself dominating conversations or having trouble letting others speak?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure. I mean, I talk a lot sometimes, especially if it’s about work or something I’m trying to explain, but I don’t know if I’m dominating. My wife might have a different answer to that.\n\nLike with the drivers, I can get going about routes or customers and then I realize I’ve been explaining the whole thing when they probably just needed the short version. But… I don’t know, I’ve always kind of done that some. So I’m not sure if it’s more than normal or not.\n\nDoctor: Do you find that others often interrupt you or tell you that you're talking too much when you're with friends or family?\n\nPatient: I don’t know that they interrupt me a lot, exactly. My wife will kind of give me a look sometimes, like, “wrap it up,” but she’s always done that when I get going on work stuff.\n\nWith friends or family… I mean, I’m usually just trying to explain what I mean. Sometimes the story takes a while because there’s background to it—like if I’m talking about a delivery problem, you have to understand the route, the customer, who was driving that day… but I don’t know if people are actually telling me I talk too much. I haven’t really noticed it like that.\n\nDoctor: Have you had to stop or significantly cut back on any activities or responsibilities because of how you've been feeling lately?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, I haven’t stopped working or anything, but I’ve had to lean on people more than I like. At work, dispatch has had to repeat details to me that I should’ve caught the first time, and a couple clients too. I’m supposed to be helping keep things steady, and instead I can tell I’m adding strain. That’s been hard to look at.\n\nDoctor: Do you often find yourself talking excessively with friends or family, to the point where they seem uninterested or try to change the subject?\n\nPatient: I don’t know… maybe sometimes, but I don’t really catch it in the moment. If I’m telling my brother something about work, I’ll get into all the details, because to me the details matter. Like if you leave out one part of a route change, the whole thing doesn’t make sense, you know?\n\nMy wife might change the subject sometimes, but she does that when I’m going on about the same work problem for too long. My wife and my daughter, I can tell they’re kind of careful around me sometimes, like they don’t want to set me off or add one more thing. I’ll start on one thing, then think about another route, then something I need to fix at home—like the fence line or whatever—and suddenly I haven’t finished the first thing.\n\nAt home it’s more… I don’t know, it’s not just chores. My wife and daughter seem like they’re walking on eggshells sometimes, and I can tell I’m adding strain when I’m supposed to be helping. So yeah, it’s getting in the way some.\n\nDoctor: Have you been engaging in more physical activity than usual lately?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I guess so. Not like I’m going to the gym or anything, but I’m moving around more. I keep finding extra jobs for myself—messing with route plans, checking in on new drivers after hours, stuff like that.\n\nAnd then at home I’ll notice one thing that needs fixing, then another… like I can’t just sit on the couch without feeling antsy. My wife will be trying to watch something and I’m up looking for a tool or walking around thinking about what else I should be doing. It’s not always bad, but it’s more than usual, yeah.\n\nDoctor: When did you first notice these changes in your behavior and thinking?\n\nPatient: I think it started… maybe around seven months ago? It’s hard to pin down exactly, because things kind of piled up, you know. My daughter moved back home, and I was trying to keep everything steady for everybody—work, the house, family stuff.\n\nAt first I figured I was just busy and doing what needed doing, but looking back, that’s probably when my behavior and thinking started changing. Around then.\n\nDoctor: Do you often find yourself dominating conversations or having trouble letting others speak?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure. I mean, I talk a lot sometimes, especially if it’s about work or something I’m trying to explain, but I don’t know if I’m dominating. My wife might have a different answer to that.\n\nLike with the drivers, I can get going about routes or customers and then I realize I’ve been explaining the whole thing when they probably just needed the short version. But… I don’t know, I’ve always kind of done that some. So I’m not sure if it’s more than normal or not.\n\nDoctor: Do you find that others often interrupt you or tell you that you're talking too much when you're with friends or family?\n\nPatient: I don’t know that they interrupt me a lot, exactly. My wife will kind of give me a look sometimes, like, “wrap it up,” but she’s always done that when I get going on work stuff.\n\nWith friends or family… I mean, I’m usually just trying to explain what I mean. Sometimes the story takes a while because there’s background to it—like if I’m talking about a delivery problem, you have to understand the route, the customer, who was driving that day… but I don’t know if people are actually telling me I talk too much. I haven’t really noticed it like that.\n\nDoctor: Have you had to stop or significantly cut back on any activities or responsibilities because of how you've been feeling lately?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, I haven’t stopped working or anything, but I’ve had to lean on people more than I like. At work, dispatch has had to repeat details to me that I should’ve caught the first time, and a couple clients too. That’s embarrassing, because usually I’m the guy who remembers all the little pieces—who wants what, which stop needs the side door, all that.\n\nAnd I did let a couple of sales follow-ups slip, not because I didn’t care, but because I was chasing too many things at once, trying to fix this route or think of some new idea, and then the simple thing didn’t get done.\n\nAt home… I don’t know if I’d call it cutting back, exactly, but my wife and daughter seem like they’re walking on eggshells some days. I’m supposed to be helping keep things steady, and instead I can tell I’m adding strain. That’s been hard to look at.\n\nDoctor: Do you often find yourself talking excessively with friends or family, to the point where they seem uninterested or try to change the subject?\n\nPatient: I don’t know… maybe sometimes, but I don’t really catch it in the moment. If I’m telling my brother something about work, I’ll get into all the details, because to me the details matter. Like if you leave out one part of a route change, the whole thing doesn’t make sense, you know?\n\nMy wife might change the subject sometimes, but she does that when I’m going on about the same work problem for too long. I can’t always tell if people are uninterested or if they just don’t care about delivery stuff the way I do. So… I wouldn’t say I notice it a lot, but maybe other people would.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any significant problems in your daily life due to these distractions and restlessness?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I guess there have been some problems. I don’t want to make it sound like everything’s falling apart, because I’m still showing up and doing what I’m supposed to do, but… at work I’ve had dispatch repeat things to me that I should’ve caught. Clients too, sometimes, and that bothers me because I’m usually pretty good with details.\n\nAnd then a couple sales follow-ups slipped through because I was off chasing three other ideas at once—like trying to fix a route issue, thinking about a customer thing, then something else pops up—and the basic stuff doesn’t get finished.\n\nAt home, that’s probably where I feel it more. My wife and my daughter, I can tell they’re kind of careful around me sometimes, like they don’t want to set me off or add one more thing. I’m supposed to be the steady one, or at least helping, and lately I feel like I’m adding strain instead.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in how your wife and daughter interact with you since you started feeling this way?\n\nPatient: Yeah… they have, I think. My wife especially, she kind of measures what she says before she says it. And my daughter too—she’ll get quiet if I seem wound up about something.\n\nIt’s not like they’re avoiding me completely, but it feels like they’re walking on eggshells some days. And that’s hard, because I’m supposed to be helping keep the house steady, not making people tiptoe around me.\n\nI notice it more after work, maybe because I come home still thinking about everything I missed or need to fix—like dispatch having to repeat things, or a client reminding me of details I should’ve had straight. Then I’m chasing ten ideas in my head, and a simple sales follow-up slips through, and I bring that tension home. So yeah… I can tell I’m adding strain.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your relationships with friends or colleagues at work due to your recent behavior?\n\nPatient: Yeah… some at work, I guess. It’s hard to pin down exactly, because things kind of piled up, you know. My daughter moved back home, and I was trying to keep everything steady for everybody—work, the house, family stuff.\n\nAt first I figured I was just busy and doing what needed doing, but looking back, that’s probably when my behavior and thinking started changing. Around then.\n\nDoctor: Do you often find yourself dominating conversations or having trouble letting others speak?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure. I mean, I talk a lot sometimes, especially if it’s about work or something I’m trying to explain, but I don’t know if I’m dominating. My wife might have a different answer to that.\n\nLike with the drivers, I can get going about routes or customers and then I realize I’ve been explaining the whole thing when they probably just needed the short version. But… I don’t know, I’ve always kind of done that some. So I’m not sure if it’s more than normal or not.\n\nDoctor: Do you find that others often interrupt you or tell you that you're talking too much when you're with friends or family?\n\nPatient: I don’t know that they interrupt me a lot, exactly. My wife will kind of give me a look sometimes, like, “wrap it up,” but she’s always done that when I get going on work stuff.\n\nWith friends or family… I mean, I’m usually just trying to explain what I mean. Sometimes the story takes a while because there’s background to it—like if I’m talking about a delivery problem, you have to understand the route, the customer, who was driving that day… but I don’t know if people are actually telling me I talk too much. I haven’t really noticed it like that.\n\nDoctor: Have you had to stop or significantly cut back on any activities or responsibilities because of how you've been feeling lately?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, I haven’t stopped working or anything, but I’ve had to lean on people more than I like. At work, dispatch has had to repeat details to me that I should’ve caught the first time, and a couple clients too. That’s embarrassing, because usually I’m the guy who remembers all the little pieces—who wants what, which stop needs the side door, all that.\n\nAnd I did let a couple of sales follow-ups slip, not because I didn’t care, but because I was chasing too many things at once, trying to fix this route or think of some new idea, and then the simple thing didn’t get done.\n\nAt home… I don’t know if I’d call it cutting back, exactly, but my wife and daughter seem like they’re walking on eggshells some days. I’m supposed to be helping keep things steady, and instead I can tell I’m adding strain. That’s been hard to look at.\n\nDoctor: Do you often find yourself talking excessively with friends or family, to the point where they seem uninterested or try to change the subject?\n\nPatient: I don’t know… maybe sometimes, but I don’t really catch it in the moment. If I’m telling my brother something about work, I’ll get into all the details, because to me the details matter. Like if you leave out one part of a route change, the whole thing doesn’t make sense, you know?\n\nMy wife might change the subject sometimes, but she does that when I’m going on about the same work problem for too long. I can’t always tell if people are uninterested or if they just don’t care about delivery stuff the way I do. So… I wouldn’t say I notice it a lot, but maybe other people would.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any significant problems in your daily life due to these distractions and restlessness?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I guess there have been some problems. I don’t want to make it sound like everything’s falling apart, because I’m still showing up and doing what I’m supposed to do, but… at work I’ve had dispatch repeat things to me that I should’ve caught. Clients too, sometimes, and that bothers me because I’m usually pretty good with details.\n\nAnd then a couple sales follow-ups slipped through because I was off chasing three other ideas at once—like trying to fix a route issue, thinking about a customer thing, then something else pops up—and the basic stuff doesn’t get finished.\n\nAt home, that’s probably where I feel it more. My wife and my daughter, I can tell they’re kind of careful around me sometimes, like they don’t want to set me off or add one more thing. I’m supposed to be the steady one, or at least helping, and lately I feel like I’m adding strain instead.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in how your wife and daughter interact with you since you started feeling this way?\n\nPatient: Yeah… they have, I think. My wife especially, she kind of measures what she says before she says it. And my daughter too—she’ll get quiet if I seem wound up about something.\n\nIt’s not like they’re avoiding me completely, but it feels like they’re walking on eggshells some days. And that’s hard, because I’m supposed to be helping keep the house steady, not making people tiptoe around me.\n\nI notice it more after work, maybe because I come home still thinking about everything I missed or need to fix—like dispatch having to repeat things, or a client reminding me of details I should’ve had straight. Then I’m chasing ten ideas in my head, and a simple sales follow-up slips through, and I bring that tension home. So yeah… I can tell I’m adding strain.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your relationships with friends or colleagues at work due to your recent behavior?\n\nPatient: Yeah… some at work, I guess. I mean, I still get along with people, it’s not like anybody’s yelling at me or anything, but dispatch has had to repeat things to me that I should’ve caught the first time. And a couple clients too, which is embarrassing because that’s kind of my job, you know, keeping those details straight.\n\nIt’s like I’ll be thinking about one route change, then a customer thing, then something I meant to follow up on, and then—actually this happened with a sales follow-up—I just let it slip because I was chasing too many ideas at once.\n\nWith friends, I don’t know… I haven’t really been seeing people much besides work and home. At home is where I notice it more. My wife and my daughter, they’re careful around me lately, like they’re walking on eggshells. And I hate that, because I’m supposed to be helping keep things steady, not making everybody tense.\n\nDoctor: Have you felt like you're unable to fulfill your responsibilities at work or at home due to your recent behavior and thoughts?\n\nPatient: Yeah… not completely unable, I wouldn’t say that. I still go to work, I still come home and try to do what needs doing. But I can tell I’m not carrying it the way I usually do.\n\nAt work, dispatch has had to repeat things to me—stuff I should’ve tracked the first time. Clients too, which gets under my skin because details matter in what I do. And a couple sales follow-ups slipped through because I was off chasing too many ideas at once, like one thing leads to another and then I forget the thing I was actually supposed to finish.\n\nAnd at home… yeah, that’s where it hits me. My wife and daughter are kind of careful around me lately, like they’re walking on eggshells. I’m supposed to be helping keep things steady, not making the house feel more strained. So, yeah, I’m still functioning, but I’m definitely not doing it like I should.\n\nDoctor: Have you felt like you're unable to enjoy activities you normally like due to your recent behavior and thoughts?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure I’d put it that way, exactly. I mean… I still do things I normally like, or at least I try to. It’s more like I don’t always settle into them the same way.\n\nLike, I’ll sit down to watch something or mess around in the garage a little, and then my mind goes off to some work thing, or I remember something I was supposed to do, and then I’m not really in it anymore. But I don’t know if that means I’m unable to enjoy it, you know?\n\nSometimes I think maybe I’m just tired from the day. My dad used to say you don’t get to enjoy anything until the work’s handled, and I guess that sticks with you. So… yeah, I don’t know. I haven’t really been paying attention to it like that.\n\nDoctor: Have you felt like your recent behavior and thoughts have significantly impacted your ability to function at work or at home?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, “significantly” sounds like a big word, but it has affected things. At work, I’m still showing up and doing the job, but dispatch has had to repeat details to me that I should’ve caught. Clients too, which—honestly—that bothers me, because if you miss one little thing in this kind of work, it can turn into a whole mess.\n\nAnd I had a couple sales follow-ups slip through. Not because I didn’t care, just because my mind was jumping around, chasing too many things at once. Like I’d think of one thing, then another, and then the thing I actually needed to finish was gone.\n\nAt home, it’s… I don’t know, it’s harder to admit. My wife and my daughter have been kind of walking on eggshells around me. I can tell I’m adding strain to the house, when I’m supposed to be the one helping keep things steady. So yeah, I guess it has impacted me. Not like everything’s fallen apart, but enough that I notice it.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. Yeah… I guess it has been more than six months. About seven months, if I’m being honest.

Some days I probably run on a bit, especially if I’m trying to explain something or tell a story, but I don’t know that I’ve noticed it as a big change.\n\nMy wife might have a better answer than me on that. She notices that stuff before I do.\n\nDoctor: Are you having trouble completing tasks at work or at home because of these distractions and feelings of restlessness?\n\nPatient: Yeah… yeah, I think so. At work especially, I’ll miss something I normally would’ve kept straight. Like dispatch will tell me details, or a client will give me something, and then I’m asking them to repeat it because my head’s already gone three other places.\n\nAnd I’ve let a couple sales follow-ups slip through, which isn’t like me. I’ll start on one thing, then think about another route, then something I need to fix at home—like the fence line or whatever—and suddenly I haven’t finished the first thing.\n\nAt home it’s more… I don’t know, it’s not just chores. My wife and daughter seem like they’re walking on eggshells sometimes, and I can tell I’m adding strain when I’m supposed to be helping. So yeah, it’s getting in the way some.\n\nDoctor: Have you been engaging in more physical activity than usual lately?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I guess so. Not like I’m going to the gym or anything, but I’m moving around more. I keep finding extra jobs for myself—messing with route plans, checking in on new drivers after hours, stuff like that.\n\nAnd then at home I’ll notice one thing that needs fixing, then another… like I can’t just sit on the couch without feeling antsy. My wife will be trying to watch something and I’m up looking for a tool or walking around thinking about what else I should be doing. It’s not always bad, but it’s more than usual, yeah.\n\nDoctor: When did you first notice these changes in your behavior and thinking?\n\nPatient: I think it started… maybe around seven months ago? It’s hard to pin down exactly, because things kind of piled up, you know. My daughter moved back home, and I was trying to keep everything steady for everybody—work, the house, family stuff.\n\nAt first I figured I was just busy and doing what needed doing, but looking back, that’s probably when my behavior and thinking started changing. Around then.\n\nDoctor: Do you often find yourself dominating conversations or having trouble letting others speak?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure. I mean, I talk a lot sometimes, especially if it’s about work or something I’m trying to explain, but I don’t know if I’m dominating. My wife might have a different answer to that.\n\nLike with the drivers, I can get going about routes or customers and then I realize I’ve been explaining the whole thing when they probably just needed the short version. But… I don’t know, I’ve always kind of done that some. So I’m not sure if it’s more than normal or not.\n\nDoctor: Do you find that others often interrupt you or tell you that you're talking too much when you're with friends or family?\n\nPatient: I don’t know that they interrupt me a lot, exactly. My wife will kind of give me a look sometimes, like, “wrap it up,” but she’s always done that when I get going on work stuff.\n\nWith friends or family… I mean, I’m usually just trying to explain what I mean. Sometimes the story takes a while because there’s background to it—like if I’m talking about a delivery problem, you have to understand the route, the customer, who was driving that day… but I don’t know if people are actually telling me I talk too much. I haven’t really noticed it like that.\n\nDoctor: Have you had to stop or significantly cut back on any activities or responsibilities because of how you've been feeling lately?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, I haven’t stopped working or anything, but I’ve had to lean on people more than I like. At work, dispatch has had to repeat details to me that I should’ve caught the first time, and a couple clients too. That’s embarrassing, because usually I’m the guy who remembers all the little pieces—who wants what, which stop needs the side door, all that.\n\nAnd I did let a couple of sales follow-ups slip, not because I didn’t care, but because I was chasing too many things at once, trying to fix this route or think of some new idea, and then the simple thing didn’t get done.\n\nAt home… I don’t know if I’d call it cutting back, exactly, but my wife and daughter seem like they’re walking on eggshells some days. I’m supposed to be helping keep things steady, and instead I can tell I’m adding strain. That’s been hard to look at.\n\nDoctor: Do you often find yourself talking excessively with friends or family, to the point where they seem uninterested or try to change the subject?\n\nPatient: I don’t know… maybe sometimes, but I don’t really catch it in the moment. If I’m telling my brother something about work, I’ll get into all the details, because to me the details matter. Like if you leave out one part of a route change, the whole thing doesn’t make sense, you know?\n\nMy wife might change the subject sometimes, but she does that when I’m going on about the same work problem for too long. I can’t always tell if people are uninterested or if they just don’t care about delivery stuff the way I do. My wife and my daughter, I can tell they’re kind of careful around me sometimes, like they don’t want to set me off or add one more thing. I’m supposed to be the steady one, or at least helping, and lately I feel like I’m adding strain instead.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in how your wife and daughter interact with you since you started feeling this way?\n\nPatient: Yeah… they have, I think. My wife especially, she kind of measures what she says before she says it. Then I’m chasing ten ideas in my head, and a simple sales follow-up slips through, and I bring that tension home. My wife and daughter are kind of careful around me lately, like they’re walking on eggshells. I’m supposed to be helping keep things steady, not making the house feel more strained. But I don’t know if that means I’m unable to enjoy it, you know?\n\nSometimes I think maybe I’m just tired from the day. My dad used to say you don’t get to enjoy anything until the work’s handled, and I guess that sticks with you. So… yeah, I don’t know. I haven’t really been paying attention to it like that.\n\nDoctor: Have you felt like your recent behavior and thoughts have significantly impacted your ability to function at work or at home?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, “significantly” sounds like a big word, but it has affected things. Clients too, which—honestly—that bothers me, because if you miss one little thing in this kind of work, it can turn into a whole mess.\n\nAnd I had a couple sales follow-ups slip through.
